# v1.6 Track E — Settings and Environment Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Give a model control of the environment a drawing lives in — ISO-25/ANSI dimension, text and leader styles, a grown `drawing_settings` facade with a system-variable catalogue, page setup with PDF-verified plotting and five bundled templates, multiple documents, portable layer states, named views and UCS, and (live only) launching, preferences, document properties and operator prompts — as 38 tools, plus the Track A hardening fixes.

**Architecture:** Three new contract modules (`styles`, `pagesetup`, `environment`), authored standards data under `engineering/standards/`, a portable layer-state codec under `engineering/environment/`, three new `server.py` sections (18 Styles, 19 Page setup & templates, 20 Environment). The ezdxf backend gains a document registry so `document_*` works headlessly. COM-only members use `@capability` and both capability maps declare the keys.

**Tech Stack:** Python 3.11+, FastMCP 3, ezdxf 1.4, pywin32 (fake-tested + live smoke), pytest-asyncio.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-16-v1.6-settings-design.md` (parent `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-15-v1.6-roadmap-design.md`, track E). Track A's plan (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-15-v1.6-pid-implementation.md`) is the style reference: every task = failing test → run → implement → run → gates → commit.

## Global Constraints

- Run every command through `uv run --frozen ...` (extras already installed; never `uv sync`).
- The user's principle for this track: tool count is not the goal. Every tool: `@cad_tool(summary=, cost=)` above `@mcp.tool(...)`, a rich alias record (`discovery/aliases.py`: AutoCAD command names + shop-floor synonyms), a docstring that names its refusals, membership in a pack (`core` or `settings`), and an explicit `lean` decision (§8.2 of the spec). No near-duplicate registrations.
- Every tool-adding task: regenerate `docs/tool-inventory.json` (`uv run --frozen python scripts/generate_tool_inventory.py`), bump the README release-snapshot tool count, keep the SECTION header count right, bump the frozen snapshot in `tests/test_tool_registry.py::test_tool_group_sizes_are_unchanged` (precedent: Track A tasks 3/4/11/13/14/17), and run the gates (`tests/test_discovery_aliases.py`, `tests/test_tool_inventory.py`, `tests/test_release_consistency.py`, `tests/test_tool_registry.py`, `tests/test_capability_contract.py`).
- Validation before any write; refusals are `ValueError`/`TypeError` naming the key — with one standing exception: a symbol *name* (block, layer, and from S Task 10 dimstyle / textstyle / mleaderstyle) that fails `security.sanitize_symbol_name` raises `ToolError`, exactly as the existing block and layer tools do; COM paths fake-tested with the `tests/test_block_define.py` `_Recorder` pattern and executed live once by `scripts/smoke_settings_com.py` (Task 24).
- Commit per task with the task's message and the two attribution lines (`Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>`, `Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01FKFStjEFzpGyLq5bMpWxTd`); use `-c user.name="Umut CAN" -c user.email="umutcan.edizaslan@gmail.com"` if git asks for an identity.
- Worktree groups (see §Execution): tasks inside one group are sequential; groups run in parallel worktrees and are merged by a merge agent that regenerates the three count files. A task must therefore never touch another group's files.

## Interfaces (the contract every task builds to — copy signatures verbatim)

### Styles (`backends/contracts/styles.py`, group S)

```python
class StylesContract(ABC):
    @abstractmethod
    async def dimstyle_list(self) -> list[dict]: ...
    # [{"name", "current": bool, "values": {DIMTXT, DIMASZ, DIMEXE, DIMEXO, DIMGAP, DIMTAD, DIMTIH, DIMTOH,
    #    DIMDEC, DIMDSEP, DIMLUNIT, DIMZIN, DIMBLK, DIMTXSTY, DIMLWD, DIMLWE, DIMSCALE}}]
    @abstractmethod
    async def dimstyle_create(self, name: str, values: dict, set_current: bool = False) -> dict: ...
    # values: the fully resolved dict from engineering.standards.dimstyles.resolve_dimstyle(preset, overrides)
    # → {"ok": True, "name", "values", "current": bool, "textstyle_created": bool}
    @abstractmethod
    async def dimstyle_modify(self, name: str, values: dict) -> dict: ...
    # → {"ok", "name", "changed": {VAR: [old, new]}, "dimensions_using_style": [handles], "rerender_required": bool}
    @abstractmethod
    async def dimstyle_set_current(self, name: str) -> dict: ...  # → {"ok", "current", "previous"}
    @abstractmethod
    async def textstyle_list(self) -> list[dict]: ...
    # [{"name", "font", "height", "width_factor", "oblique_deg", "current": bool}]
    @abstractmethod
    async def textstyle_create(self, name: str, font: str, height: float = 0.0, width_factor: float = 1.0,
                               oblique_deg: float = 0.0, set_current: bool = False) -> dict: ...
    # → {"ok", "name", "font", "font_resolved": bool, "current"}
    @abstractmethod
    async def textstyle_set_current(self, name: str) -> dict: ...
    @abstractmethod
    async def mleaderstyle_list(self) -> list[dict]: ...  # [{"name", "arrow_size", "landing_gap", "text_style", "text_height"}]
    @capability("mleaderstyle", reason="ActiveX exposes no MLeaderStyle collection; use leader_create_mleader's per-leader parameters")
    async def mleaderstyle_create(self, name: str, values: dict) -> dict: ...  # ezdxf only
```

`engineering/standards/dimstyles.py`: `PRESETS = {"iso-25": {...}, "ansi": {...}}` (values exactly as spec §4.1, DIMDSEP as a one-character string), `DIM_VARIABLE_WHITELIST: frozenset`, `DIM_VARIABLE_RANGES: dict[str, tuple|set]`, `resolve_dimstyle(preset: str | None, overrides: dict | None) -> dict` (raises `ValueError` naming the key on unknown/out-of-range; preset `None` means the ISO-25 base with overrides), `describe_preset(name) -> dict`.
`engineering/standards/textstyles.py`: `TEXT_PRESETS = {"ISOCP": ("isocp.shx", 1.0, 0.0), "ISOCPEUR": ("isocpeur.ttf", 1.0, 0.0), "ARIAL": ("arial.ttf", 1.0, 0.0), "ROMANS": ("romans.shx", 1.0, 0.0)}`, `resolve_font(font) -> (font_file, known: bool)`.
`engineering/standards/mleaderstyles.py`: `MLEADER_PRESETS = {"iso": {"arrow_size": 2.5, "landing_gap": 1.0, "text_style": "ISOCP", "text_height": 2.5}, "ansi": {"arrow_size": 3.0, "landing_gap": 1.5, "text_style": "ROMANS", "text_height": 3.0}}`, `resolve_mleaderstyle(preset, overrides)`.
`engineering/standards/apply.py`: `async def apply_standard(backend, standard: str, layers: bool = True, units: bool = True) -> dict` → `{"standard", "dimstyle": {"name", "created", "current": True}, "textstyle": {"name", "created", "current": True}, "settings": {"changed": {VAR: [old, new]}, "applied": bool}, "layers": {"layer_set", "layers": {name: "created"|"exists"|"failed: ..."}} | None}` — the shape S Task 12 ships and its tests pin (`settings` is the two-key dict `{changed, applied}`, not the bare `changed` map); also `STANDARDS`, `STANDARD_SYSVARS`, `resolve_standard(standard) -> (key, row)`.

### Settings (group C)

- `backends/contracts/settings.py`: `_SETTING_MAP` gains `limits` (kind `"limits"` → `$LIMMIN`/`$LIMMAX`), `grid` (`"bool"` → GRIDMODE), `grid_spacing` (`"spacing"` → GRIDUNIT, both axes), `snap` (`"bool"` → SNAPMODE), `snap_spacing` (`"spacing"` → SNAPUNIT), `ortho` (`"bool"` → ORTHOMODE), `polar` (`"bit"` → **AUTOSNAP bit 8** — measured by C Task 14: the F10 toggle is AUTOSNAP bit 8, POLARMODE bit 0 only selects relative polar angles, so the spec's "POLARMODE bit 0" is corrected), `polar_angle` (`"degrees"` → POLARANG, stored in radians), `psltscale` (`"bool"` → PSLTSCALE), `annotation_scale` (`"scale"` → CANNOSCALE `"1:50"`; CANNOSCALEVALUE 0.02 is derived and read-only), `linear_units` (`"lunits"` enum → LUNITS 1–5), `angular_units` (`"aunits"` enum → AUNITS 0–4), `dimstyle` (`"name"` → `dimstyle_set_current`), `textstyle` (`"name"` → `textstyle_set_current`). `drawing_settings(get)` reports every key; ranges in `_SETTING_RANGES`; `parse_scale(text) -> (name, paper, drawing)` and `scale_value(text) -> float` are module-level (the ezdxf backend imports them).
- `engineering/standards/sysvars.py`: `SYSVAR_CATALOG: dict[str, SysVar]` — 90 authored rows (`SysVar(name, type: "int"|"float"|"str"|"bool"|"point"|"enum", range: tuple|None, enum: dict|None, default, meaning, saved_in: "drawing"|"registry"|"not_saved", engines: {"ezdxf": bool, "com": bool}, friendly_key: str|None, read_only: bool = False)` — `read_only` is the one field beyond the spec's signature, trailing and defaulted: DIMSTYLE, CANNOSCALEVALUE, UCSNAME, VIEWCTR, VIEWSIZE, DWGNAME, DWGPREFIX are read-only in AutoCAD and `check_sysvar_value` needs to know), `describe_sysvar(name) -> dict` (`known: False` + `nearest: [names]` on miss), `search_sysvars(text) -> list[dict]`, `check_sysvar_value(name, value) -> None | str` (message when out of range; `None` when fine or unknown).
- `server.py`: `system_variable_describe(name=None, search=None)`; `system_set_variable` calls `check_sysvar_value` first and refuses with its message.
- `backends/contracts/environment.py` (this group owns these two members; group V owns the rest of the module — coordinate by adding them in a clearly marked `# ── properties ──` block):

```python
    @abstractmethod
    async def drawing_properties_get(self) -> dict: ...
    # → {"summary": {"title","subject","author","keywords","comments"} (None each headless), "summary_available": bool, "custom": {k: v}}
    @abstractmethod
    async def drawing_properties_set(self, summary: dict | None = None, custom: dict | None = None) -> dict: ...
    # ezdxf: summary with any non-None field → raise UnsupportedCapabilityError("dwgprops"); custom → header custom_vars (replace keys given; value None deletes)
    # → {"ok", "summary_written": [...], "custom_written": [...], "custom_deleted": [...]}
```

### Page setup and templates (`backends/contracts/pagesetup.py`, group P)

```python
class PageSetupContract(ABC):
    @abstractmethod
    async def page_setup_list(self, layout: str | None = None) -> list[dict]: ...
    # [{"layout", "paper", "size_mm": [w, h], "orientation", "plot_style", "scale", "plot_area", "device", "margins_mm": [t, b, l, r], "center": bool}]
    @abstractmethod
    async def page_setup_apply(self, layout: str, setup: dict) -> dict: ...
    # setup: resolved by engineering.standards.papers.resolve_page_setup(paper, orientation, plot_style, scale, plot_area, device, margins_mm, center)
    # → {"ok", "layout", "applied": {...setup}, "changed": {key: [old, new]}, "plot_style_known": bool}
    @abstractmethod
    async def plot_style_list(self) -> list[dict]: ...  # [{"name", "source": "catalog"|"installed"}]
    @abstractmethod
    async def drawing_template_save(self, path: str, name: str | None = None, description: str | None = None) -> dict: ...
    # ezdxf: suffix .dxf writes; suffix .dwt → raise UnsupportedCapabilityError("dwt_write"); COM: SaveAs(path, acTemplateDwg) for .dwt, DXF export for .dxf
    # → {"ok", "path", "format": "dxf"|"dwt", "backend"}
```

`engineering/standards/papers.py`: `PAPER_SIZES = {"ISO_A0": (841, 1189), "ISO_A1": (594, 841), "ISO_A2": (420, 594), "ISO_A3": (297, 420), "ISO_A4": (210, 297), "ANSI_A": (216, 279), "ANSI_B": (279, 432), "ANSI_C": (432, 559), "ANSI_D": (559, 864), "ANSI_E": (864, 1118)}` (portrait w×h; landscape swaps), `CTB_CATALOG = ("monochrome.ctb", "acad.ctb", "Grayscale.ctb", "Screening 100%.ctb", "Screening 75%.ctb", "Screening 50%.ctb", "Screening 25%.ctb", "Fill Patterns.ctb", "DWF Virtual Pens.ctb")`, `SCALES = {"fit": None, "1:1": 1.0, "1:2": 0.5, "1:5": 0.2, "1:10": 0.1, "1:20": 0.05, "1:50": 0.02, "1:100": 0.01, "2:1": 2.0, "5:1": 5.0, "10:1": 10.0}`, `resolve_page_setup(paper, orientation="landscape", plot_style="monochrome.ctb", scale="fit", plot_area="layout", device="DWG To PDF.pc3", margins_mm=None, center=True) -> dict` (raises `ValueError` naming the field), `canonical_media_name(paper, orientation) -> str` (`"ISO_A3_(420.00_x_297.00_MM)"` — AutoCAD's spelling: landscape A3 is 420×297).
`engineering/standards/pdfinfo.py`: `read_mediabox(path) -> tuple[float, float]` in mm (parses the first `/MediaBox [x0 y0 x1 y1]`, points → mm ×25.4/72; raises `ValueError` when absent).
`engineering/standards/templates.py`: `TEMPLATE_CATALOG` (five rows as spec §6), `resolve_template(name_or_path: str, engine: str) -> tuple[str, str]` (`(path, "bundled"|"path")`; bundled = `templates/<name>.dxf` for ezdxf, `templates/<name>.dwt` for com, falling back to the `.dxf` with `source: "bundled_dxf"` when the `.dwt` is missing), `TEMPLATES_DIR`.
Server-level compositions in `engineering/standards/plot.py`: `async def batch_plot(backend, layouts, output_dir, file_pattern="{drawing}-{layout}.pdf") -> dict` → `{ok, drawing, output_dir, format: "pdf", sheets: [{layout, ok, path, bytes, mediabox_mm: [w, h] | None, paper: str | None, error?}], count}` — the per-sheet list is under **`sheets`** (P Task 18's key, pinned by its tests and tool docstring; F Task 25 reads `report["sheets"][0]`), each row's `mediabox_mm` from `read_mediabox`; `safe_filename(text) -> str`.

### Environment (`backends/contracts/environment.py`, group V)

```python
class EnvironmentContract(ABC):
    @abstractmethod
    async def document_list(self) -> list[dict]: ...  # [{"name", "path", "active": bool, "saved": bool, "entity_count"}]
    @abstractmethod
    async def document_activate(self, name_or_path: str) -> dict: ...  # → {"ok", "active", "previous"}
    @abstractmethod
    async def document_close(self, name_or_path: str | None = None, save: bool = False, discard: bool = False) -> dict: ...
    # unsaved and not save and not discard → ValueError naming the document; → {"ok", "closed", "saved": bool, "active": name|None}
    @abstractmethod
    async def layer_state_save(self, name: str, description: str | None = None) -> dict: ...  # → {"ok", "name", "layer_count", "replaced"}
    @abstractmethod
    async def layer_state_restore(self, name: str, properties: list[str] | None = None) -> dict: ...
    # properties ⊆ {"on","frozen","locked","color","linetype","lineweight","plot","current"}; → {"ok", "name", "applied", "missing_layers", "new_layers"}
    @abstractmethod
    async def layer_state_list(self) -> list[dict]: ...  # [{"name", "description", "layer_count"}]
    @abstractmethod
    async def layer_state_delete(self, name: str) -> dict: ...
    @abstractmethod
    async def view_named_save(self, name: str, center=None, height: float | None = None, width: float | None = None) -> dict: ...
    # → {"ok", "name", "center": [x, y], "height", "width", "replaced"}
    @abstractmethod
    async def view_named_restore(self, name: str) -> dict: ...  # ezdxf: sets $VIEWCTR/$VIEWSIZE, "applied": "header_only"
    @abstractmethod
    async def view_named_list(self) -> list[dict]: ...
    @abstractmethod
    async def ucs_list(self) -> list[dict]: ...  # [{"name", "origin", "x_axis", "y_axis", "current": bool}] + the implicit "world"
    @abstractmethod
    async def ucs_set(self, name: str, origin, x_axis, y_axis) -> dict: ...  # non-orthogonal → ValueError with the measured angle
    @abstractmethod
    async def ucs_restore(self, name: str) -> dict: ...  # "world" resets
    @capability("live_application", reason="only a live CAD application can be launched or attached")
    async def system_launch(self, visible: bool = True, open_path: str | None = None) -> dict: ...
    # → {"launched": bool, "attached": bool, "version", "document"}
    @capability("preferences", reason="AutoCAD preferences live in the running application, not in a file")
    async def preferences_get(self, keys: list[str] | None = None) -> dict: ...  # → {"values": {key: value}, "read_only": [keys]}
    @capability("preferences", reason=...)
    async def preferences_set(self, key: str, value) -> dict: ...  # → {"ok", "key", "old", "new"}
    @capability("interactive_prompt", reason="prompting the operator needs a live AutoCAD session")
    async def user_pick_point(self, prompt: str) -> dict: ...  # → {"cancelled": bool, "x", "y"} | {"timed_out": True}
    @capability("interactive_prompt", reason=...)
    async def user_select(self, prompt: str, mode: str = "single") -> dict: ...  # → {"cancelled", "handles": [...]}
    @capability("interactive_prompt", reason=...)
    async def system_prompt_message(self, text: str) -> dict: ...  # → {"ok"}
```

`engineering/environment/layer_states.py`: `DICT_NAME = "ACADMCP_LAYERSTATES"`, `PROPERTIES = ("on", "frozen", "locked", "color", "linetype", "lineweight", "plot", "current")`, `snapshot_from_layers(layers: list[LayerInfo], current_layer: str, *, plot: dict[str, bool] | None = None, description: str | None = None) -> dict` (the two keyword-only extensions V Task 22 ships: `LayerInfo` carries no plot flag, so the engines read `layer.dxf.plot` / `Plottable` themselves and pass it in), `encode_state(state: dict) -> list[str]` (JSON, ASCII-escaped chunks ≤ 255), `decode_state(chunks) -> dict` (`ValueError` on anything that is not a version-1 state), `diff_snapshot(state, layers) -> (missing, new)`, `validate_properties(properties) -> tuple[str, ...]`, `validate_state_name(name) -> str`.
Preference whitelist (COM): `PREFERENCE_KEYS = {"OpenSave.SaveAsType": ("enum", {...}), "OpenSave.AutoSaveInterval": ("int", (0, 600)), "OpenSave.CreateBackup": ("bool", None), "OpenSave.IncrementalSavePercent": ("int", (0, 100)), "Display.CursorSize": ("int", (1, 100)), "Drafting.AutoSnapMarkerSize": ("int", (1, 20)), "Drafting.AutoSnapTooltip": ("bool", None), "Selection.PickBoxSize": ("int", (0, 50)), "Output.DefaultPlotStyleTable": ("str", None), "Output.DefaultOutputDevice": ("str", None)}` and `READ_ONLY_KEYS = ("Files.SupportPath", "Files.TemplateDwgPath", "Files.PrintStyleSheetPath", "Files.PrinterConfigPath")` in `engineering/environment/preferences.py` (pure data + `validate_preference(key, value)`).

### Capability keys (both maps, every group's task that introduces one)

`mleaderstyle` (COM False), `dwgprops` (ezdxf False), `dwt_write` (ezdxf False), `live_application`, `preferences`, `interactive_prompt` (ezdxf False, COM True). `tests/test_capability_contract.py` fails until both maps agree. Map-only feature entries (no decorator): `registry_sysvar` (C; ezdxf False), `documents`, `named_views`, `ucs` (V; True on both) and `layer_states` (V; True on both), whose spelling is pinned by F Task 24's `test_layer_states_are_declared_as_ours_not_autocads` and quoted by the README and CLAUDE.md, so V writes it verbatim in both maps: `FeatureCapability(True, "xrecord", reason="portable_acadmcp_xrecord;not_listed_in_autocad_layer_states_manager")`.

### Packs and profile (final task)

`PACK_TOOL_NAMES["settings"]` = the 22 SECTION 20 tools; `LEAN_TOOL_NAMES` += `drawing_apply_standard`, `page_setup_apply`, `batch_plot`, `dimstyle_set_current`, `textstyle_set_current`; `TOOL_PACK_NAMES = ("core", "pid", "settings")`.

Because C and V both need `EnvironmentContract`: **V creates the module** with every member including `drawing_properties_*`; **C adds the two implementations** to both backends and registers its three tools (`system_variable_describe` in Task 15, which opens the stub at `(1 tool)`, then `drawing_properties_get/set` in Task 16) under a `# ── SECTION 20: Environment (3 tools) ──` header that V's merge extends to the full count (V ends at `(19 tools)`; merged `(22 tools)`, resolved by F Task 24 Step 3.3, which moves all three C blocks under V's header). The merge agent resolves the header count and the three count files. `backends/base.py` composition of the three new contracts is done by **whichever group merges first** (each group's first task adds its own contract to the `AutoCADBackend` bases if absent — an idempotent one-line edit).

### Merge notes (binding for every group and for the merge agent)

**Double-owned members — exactly two, no others.** (1) `drawing_template_save`: P Task 17 declares it (`@abstractmethod` in `PageSetupContract`) and gives both backends a stub that raises `UnsupportedCapabilityError("dwt_write", "drawing_template_save: not implemented yet (Task 20)")`; P Task 20 replaces both stubs with the implementations. Same group, sequential tasks — nothing for the merge agent. (2) `drawing_properties_get` / `drawing_properties_set`: V Task 21 declares them in `EnvironmentContract` under a temporary `@capability("dwgprops", reason="drawing properties arrive with group C's merge")` (so V's worktree instantiates both backends without C's code); C Task 16 implements them on both backends. **The merge agent flips V's two decorators to `@abstractmethod`** (deleting V's `# TEMPORARY` comment block) once C's implementations are present, and keeps exactly one `dwgprops` entry per capability map — C's (V's line is byte-identical to C's by construction, see below, and is deleted together with the decorators; a repeated dict key is ruff F601). Every other contract member has one declaring task and one implementing task.

**One `_active_vport`.** `EzdxfBackend._active_vport(doc)` (staticmethod: the `*Active` VPORT entry, created when absent) is defined by **C Task 14** in the `# ── settings (track E, group C) ──` block — grid/snap live on that entry — and C's definition is the one that ships. V Task 22 needs the same helper for `view_named_*` and its worktree has no C code, so V carries a byte-equivalent copy marked `# TEMPORARY (track-e/environment worktree only)`; **the merge agent deletes V's copy** (two definitions in one class are ruff F811, and the later one silently wins). V's callers (`self._active_vport(doc)`) need no change.

**Method-block anchors in `backends/ezdxf_backend.py` and `backends/com_backend.py` — one distinct, non-adjacent position per group, so the four groups never touch the same lines:**

| Group | Block banner | ezdxf anchor | COM anchor |
|---|---|---|---|
| S | `# ── styles (track E) ──` | immediately before `    # ── corner ops` (i.e. directly after `system_run_lisp`) | immediately before `    # ── corner ops` |
| C | `# ── settings (track E, group C) ──` | **at the very end of the class** — after `_create_xline`, the file's last method (below the `# ── premium meta-tools live on AutoCADBackend …` banner) | at the very end of the class, after `_create_xline` |
| V | `# ── environment (track E) ──` | immediately after the `# ── layouts / paper space ──` block, i.e. directly before `    # ── viewports ──` (after `layout_copy`) | immediately after the `# ── layouts / paper space ──` block, i.e. directly before `    # ── selection filters (M8 / F1) ──` (after `layout_copy`) |
| P | `# ── page setup (track E) ──` | immediately before `    # ── 3D solids (unsupported headlessly …)` (after the change-space block) | immediately before `    # ── 3D solids (native ActiveX; …)` (after the viewports block) |

C's in-place replacement of `system_get_variable` / `system_set_variable` (Task 14) and V's two-key edit inside `system_status` (Task 21) are both in the `# ── system ──` section but 15+ lines apart; S's block starts after `system_run_lisp`, 20+ lines below C's replacement. Later tasks of a group append inside that group's own block (C Task 16 after `_set_annotation_scale`; V Tasks 22/23 after `document_close`; P Tasks 18/20 inside the page-setup block).

**Capability-map entries (`features` dict of `capabilities()`, both backends).** In each worktree the only shared anchor that exists is the `"lisp"` entry, so S, C and V each insert directly after `"lisp"` there and the merge agent resolves that one hunk by concatenating in this order, by key name — S's entries directly after `"lisp"`, C's after S's last key, V's after C's last key: `"lisp"` → **S** `"mleaderstyle"` → **C** `"registry_sysvar"`, `"dwgprops"` → **V** `"documents"`, `"layer_states"`, `"named_views"`, `"ucs"`, `"live_application"`, `"preferences"`, `"interactive_prompt"` (V's own `"dwgprops"` line dropped, see above). **P** anchors after an existing key nobody else touches — `"paper_space"` — so its `"dwt_write"` entry never conflicts. The two `dwgprops` lines are byte-identical: V Task 21 copies C Task 16's `FeatureCapability(False, reason="summary_fields_need_live_autocad;custom_properties_supported")` (ezdxf) and `FeatureCapability(True, "native")` (COM).

**Textual overlaps that are conflicts but not decisions.** `server.py`: every group inserts its SECTION immediately before the `# ── RESOURCES` banner (after `pid_tag_parse`) — merged order is SECTION 18 (S) → SECTION 19 (P) → SECTION 20 (C's three blocks folded under V's header, F Task 24 Step 3.3); in `discovery/aliases.py` the S, P and V alias blocks all land after the P&ID block at the end of `TOOL_ALIASES` (merged order S → P → V, each block alphabetical within itself), while C edits existing records in place (`drawing_settings`, `system_variable_describe` next to `system_set_variable`, the `DWGPROPS` line of `SHARED_ACAD_COMMANDS`); the `SHARED_ACAD_COMMANDS` tuple gains S's `DIMSTYLE` / `MLEADERSTYLE` / `STYLE`, P's `PAGESETUP` / `PLOT` / `SAVEAS`, C's `DWGPROPS` and V's `CLOSE` / `LAYERSTATE` / `OPTIONS` / `UCS` / `VIEW` — alphabetical position each, so the hunks interleave but do not contradict. README "What you get" table: P Task 19 inserts a **Templates** row after **Paper space** and V Task 21 inserts an **Environment** row after **Paper space** — same insertion point; merged order is Paper space → Templates → Environment (F Task 26 later adds the Styles row and the final Environment wording). `.gitattributes`: P Task 19's `templates/*.dwt binary` and F Task 25's `*.dwt binary` overlap harmlessly — keep both. `tests/test_tool_registry.py` snapshot, `README.md` tool count, `docs/tool-inventory.json` and the SECTION 20 header count are the three-plus-one count files the merge agent re-derives (F Task 24 pins the final numbers: 204 tools, 23 groups, `(22 tools)`). `tests/data/discovery_golden.py`: S Task 13's five cases land first, F Task 24's ten after them.

# v1.6 Track E — Group H: Track A hardening (Tasks 1–8)

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Group:** H (hardening) · **Worktree:** `track-e/hardening` · **Parent plan:** `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-16-v1.6-settings-implementation.md` (its Global Constraints, File Structure, Interfaces and Execution sections are binding here) · **Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-16-v1.6-settings-design.md` §2 (wave 0) · **Baseline:** main at `ab258d1` (166 tools, 2423 collected tests).

**Goal:** Close the seven Track A review findings that are real defects — on both engines, each refused *before* any write and pinned by a test — and file every remaining minor finding with a disposition.

**Architecture:** No new tools, no new contract members except one optional kwarg (`block_define(create_layers=False)`). Shared validation lives in `backends/block_specs.py` (both engines import it); the P&ID fixes live in `engineering/pid/{lines,drawlines,spec}.py`. Headless tests run against a real ezdxf document; COM against a recording fake ActiveX surface (`tests/test_block_define.py::_Recorder` pattern, grown into `_FakeObject` / `_FakeBlocks` / `_FakeLayers` / `_FakeSpace` in the new test module).

**Tech Stack:** Python 3.11+, ezdxf 1.4.4, pywin32 (fake-tested), pytest-asyncio.

## Group constraints (in addition to the parent plan's Global Constraints)

- Run everything through `uv run --frozen ...`.
- Every fix: refusal is measured (the plan quotes the probe), named (`ValueError`/`TypeError` naming the key or path), raised before any write, and tested on both engines.
- No file owned by another group is edited. The one deliberate exception is recorded in Task 6 (`benchmarks/adapters/autocad_mcp_pro.py`, owned by the final group): the fix changes the truth that adapter asserts, so the adapter's two assertion lines move with it; the merge agent is told in the commit message.
- The README `collected tests` figure is regenerated by the merge agent (parent plan, Execution §), so every gate step below deselects `tests/test_release_consistency.py::test_document_numbers_match_the_tree`; it fails only on that figure and every other test in the file must pass.
- Tool steps keep the user's principle: the touched tools keep their alias record (grown where the fix adds vocabulary), their docstring names the refusals, and pack / `lean` membership is stated (unchanged in every task here: `block_*` and `entity_create_block_ref` are `core` and not lean; `pid_line_draw` is `pid` and lean; `pid_from_spec` is `pid` and not lean).
- Commit per task with the message given and the two attribution lines:

### Task 1: `entity_create_block_ref` refuses an undefined (or layout) block before writing, on both engines

The Track A fix (`6f4bda5`) gated `block_insert` only; its sibling `entity_create_block_ref` still returns a handle for a typo'd name and `doc.audit()` later deletes the INSERT. Both engines get one shared gate per engine, used by both placing tools, and the gate also refuses the layout blocks `*Model_Space` / `*Paper_Space` (they are in `doc.blocks`; inserting one is the reference cycle AutoCAD's INSERT refuses).

**Files:**
- Modify: `backends/ezdxf_backend.py` (new `_require_block` after `_require_doc` ≈ line 1085; `entity_create_block_ref` ≈ line 2993; `block_insert` ≈ line 4468)
- Modify: `backends/com_backend.py` (new module function `_require_block_defined` before `_BUILTIN_LINETYPES` ≈ line 195; `entity_create_block_ref` ≈ line 2471; `block_insert` ≈ line 3239)
- Modify: `server.py` (`entity_create_block_ref` docstring ≈ line 1660)
- Create: `tests/test_track_a_hardening.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: `EzdxfBackend._require_block(name) -> BlockLayout` and `com_backend._require_block_defined(doc, name) -> Block`. Both raise `TypeError("block name must be a non-empty string")`, `ValueError(f"block {name!r} is not defined")` (the message `tests/test_block_insert_attributes.py` already pins) or `ValueError(f"block {name!r} is a layout block and cannot be inserted")`, before any write.
- Consumes: ezdxf `BlockLayout.block_record.is_any_layout` (measured: `True` for `*Model_Space`, `doc.blocks` membership is case-insensitive); ActiveX `Blocks.Item(name)` raises for an unknown name, `Block.IsLayout`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests (the module's fakes serve Tasks 1–4)**

```python
# tests/test_track_a_hardening.py
"""Track A hardening (v1.6 track E, wave 0): the block-tool defects the Track A
review found, fixed on both engines.

Headless tests run against a real ezdxf document; the live engine runs against
a fake ActiveX surface that records every call, so the tests assert the call
shape (what is dispatched, in which order, and — for a refusal — that nothing
is dispatched at all).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import types

import pytest

from backends.base import EntityInfo

pytestmark = pytest.mark.asyncio


def _define_tagged_block(backend, name: str = "TB") -> None:
    blk = backend._doc.blocks.new(name=name)
    blk.add_line((0, 0), (10, 0))
    blk.add_attdef("TAG", insert=(0, 3), text="", dxfattribs={"height": 2.5})


# ── fake ActiveX surface ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────


class _FakeObject:
    """An ActiveX object with only the members it is given; property writes are recorded."""

    def __init__(self, object_name: str, handle: str, **members):
        object.__setattr__(self, "ObjectName", object_name)
        object.__setattr__(self, "Handle", handle)
        object.__setattr__(self, "writes", [])
        object.__setattr__(self, "deleted", False)
        for name, value in members.items():
            object.__setattr__(self, name, value)

    def __setattr__(self, name, value):
        self.writes.append((name, value))
        object.__setattr__(self, name, value)

    def __getattr__(self, name):
        raise AttributeError(name)

    def Delete(self):
        object.__setattr__(self, "deleted", True)


class _FakeBlock:
    def __init__(self, name: str, is_layout: bool = False):
        self.Name = name
        self.IsLayout = is_layout
        self.Count = 0
        self.calls: list[tuple[str, tuple]] = []

    def __getattr__(self, attr):
        if attr.startswith("Add"):

            def _call(*args):
                self.calls.append((attr, args))
                return _FakeObject("AcDbEntity", f"M{len(self.calls)}")

            return _call
        raise AttributeError(attr)


class _FakeBlocks:
    def __init__(self, *names: str):
        self.blocks: dict[str, _FakeBlock] = {
            "*Model_Space": _FakeBlock("*Model_Space", is_layout=True),
        }
        for name in names:
            self.blocks[name] = _FakeBlock(name)

    def Add(self, base_point, name):
        self.blocks[name] = _FakeBlock(name)
        self.blocks[name].base_point = tuple(base_point.value)
        return self.blocks[name]

    def Item(self, name):
        if name not in self.blocks:
            raise RuntimeError(f"no block {name}")
        return self.blocks[name]


class _FakeLayers:
    def __init__(self, *names: str):
        self.names = {"0", *names}
        self.added: list[str] = []

    def Item(self, name):
        if name not in self.names:
            raise RuntimeError(f"no layer {name}")
        return types.SimpleNamespace(Name=name)

    def Add(self, name):
        self.names.add(name)
        self.added.append(name)
        return types.SimpleNamespace(Name=name)


class _FakeSpace:
    """The active layout block: records InsertBlock / AddText and hands back styled objects."""

    def __init__(self, attributes: dict[str, str] | None = None):
        self.calls: list[tuple[str, tuple]] = []
        self.attributes = attributes or {}
        self.inserted: list[_FakeObject] = []

    def InsertBlock(self, point, name, sx, sy, sz, rotation):
        self.calls.append(("InsertBlock", (tuple(point.value), name, sx, sy, sz, rotation)))
        attrs = tuple(
            _FakeObject("AcDbAttribute", f"A{i}", TagString=tag, TextString=value)
            for i, (tag, value) in enumerate(self.attributes.items())
        )
        ref = _FakeObject(
            "AcDbBlockReference", f"R{len(self.inserted)}", Name=name, GetAttributes=lambda: attrs
        )
        self.inserted.append(ref)
        return ref

    def AddText(self, text, point, height):
        self.calls.append(("AddText", (text, tuple(point.value), height)))
        return _FakeObject("AcDbText", f"T{len(self.calls)}")


@pytest.fixture
def com(monkeypatch):
    """A ComBackend over a recording fake: ``document`` and ``space`` are inspectable."""
    from backends import com_backend as module

    document = types.SimpleNamespace(Blocks=_FakeBlocks("TB"), Layers=_FakeLayers(), objects={})
    document.HandleToObject = lambda handle: document.objects[handle]
    space = _FakeSpace()
    monkeypatch.setattr(module, "_acad_doc", lambda: document)
    monkeypatch.setattr(module, "_msp", lambda: space)
    monkeypatch.setattr(module, "_regen", lambda: None)
    # The real converter reads a dozen ActiveX members (bounding box, block
    # geometry, ...); the fixture only needs the handle and the name back.
    monkeypatch.setattr(
        module,
        "_entity_info",
        lambda ent: EntityInfo(
            handle=ent.Handle,
            type="INSERT",
            layer="0",
            color=256,
            linetype="ByLayer",
            visible=True,
            properties={"block_name": ent.Name},
        ),
    )
    backend = module.ComBackend()

    async def _run_inline(func, *args, **kwargs):
        return func(*args, **kwargs)

    monkeypatch.setattr(backend, "_run", _run_inline)
    return backend, document, space


# ── Task 1: entity_create_block_ref refuses an undefined block ────────────────


async def test_block_ref_of_an_undefined_block_is_refused_before_writing(backend):
    before = len(list(backend._msp()))
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="'NO_SUCH_BLOCK' is not defined"):
        await backend.entity_create_block_ref("NO_SUCH_BLOCK", 1, 2)
    assert len(list(backend._msp())) == before, "a refused insert must not write"
    assert "NO_SUCH_BLOCK" not in backend._doc.blocks


async def test_block_ref_of_a_defined_block_still_inserts(backend):
    _define_tagged_block(backend)
    ref = await backend.entity_create_block_ref("TB", 5, 5, rotation=90)
    info = await backend.entity_get(ref.handle)
    assert info.type == "INSERT" and info.properties["block_name"] == "TB"
    assert info.properties["rotation_deg"] == pytest.approx(90.0)


@pytest.mark.parametrize("name", ["*Model_Space", "*Paper_Space"])
async def test_layout_blocks_are_refused_by_both_placing_tools(backend, name):
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="layout block"):
        await backend.entity_create_block_ref(name, 0, 0)
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="layout block"):
        await backend.block_insert(name, 0, 0)
    assert backend._doc.audit().errors == [], "nothing was written, nothing to fix"


async def test_a_non_string_block_name_is_a_type_error_on_both_placing_tools(backend):
    with pytest.raises(TypeError, match="non-empty string"):
        await backend.entity_create_block_ref(None, 0, 0)
    with pytest.raises(TypeError, match="non-empty string"):
        await backend.block_insert("", 0, 0)


async def test_com_block_ref_probes_the_block_table_before_insert_block(com):
    backend, document, space = com
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="'NO_SUCH_BLOCK' is not defined"):
        await backend.entity_create_block_ref("NO_SUCH_BLOCK", 1, 2)
    assert space.calls == [], "InsertBlock must not be dispatched for an unknown name"
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="layout block"):
        await backend.entity_create_block_ref("*Model_Space", 0, 0)
    assert space.calls == []

    ref = await backend.entity_create_block_ref("TB", 1, 2, 2.0, 2.0, 90.0)
    assert ref.properties["block_name"] == "TB"
    assert space.calls == [
        ("InsertBlock", ((1.0, 2.0, 0.0), "TB", 2.0, 2.0, 1.0, pytest.approx(1.5708, abs=1e-4)))
    ]


async def test_com_block_insert_shares_the_same_gate(com):
    backend, document, space = com
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="'NO_SUCH_BLOCK' is not defined"):
        await backend.block_insert("NO_SUCH_BLOCK", 1, 2, attributes={"TAG": "P-1"})
    assert space.calls == []
```

Run: `uv run --frozen pytest tests/test_track_a_hardening.py -v`
Expected: 6 FAIL, 1 PASS. `test_block_ref_of_an_undefined_block_is_refused_before_writing`, the two `test_layout_blocks_…` cases and `test_com_block_ref_…` fail with `Failed: DID NOT RAISE ValueError` (ezdxf returns a handle; the COM fake's `InsertBlock` is dispatched and recorded); `test_a_non_string_block_name_…` fails with `ezdxf.lldxf.const.DXFValueError: Block name as string required` instead of `TypeError`; `test_com_block_insert_shares_the_same_gate` fails `DID NOT RAISE` (the fake's `InsertBlock` accepts any name). `test_block_ref_of_a_defined_block_still_inserts` passes.

- [ ] **Step 3: ezdxf — one gate for both placing tools**

In `backends/ezdxf_backend.py`, insert after `_require_doc` (directly before `def _msp(self):`):

```python
    def _require_block(self, name):
        """The block definition ``name`` names, or a refusal before any write.

        ``add_blockref`` never checks the name and ``add_auto_attribs`` skips
        autofill when ``Insert.block()`` resolves to None, so a typo'd name
        used to yield a dangling INSERT that dropped its attribute values and
        that ``doc.audit()`` later deletes. A layout block (``*Model_Space``,
        ``*Paper_Space``) is in ``doc.blocks`` too, but inserting one is the
        reference cycle AutoCAD's INSERT refuses, so it is refused here by
        name. Shared by ``block_insert`` and ``entity_create_block_ref`` so the
        two tools that place a block reference agree on what a typo does.
        """
        doc = self._require_doc()
        if not isinstance(name, str) or not name.strip():
            raise TypeError("block name must be a non-empty string")
        if name not in doc.blocks:
            raise ValueError(f"block {name!r} is not defined")
        blk = doc.blocks.get(name)
        if blk.block_record.is_any_layout:
            raise ValueError(f"block {name!r} is a layout block and cannot be inserted")
        return blk
```

In `entity_create_block_ref`, add the gate as the first statement after `msp = self._msp()`:

```python

            msp = self._msp()
            self._require_block(name)
            ent = msp.add_blockref(
```

In `backends/com_backend.py`, insert before `_BUILTIN_LINETYPES = {"continuous", "bylayer", "byblock"}`:

```python
def _require_block_defined(doc, name):
    """``doc.Blocks.Item(name)``, or a ``ValueError`` before ``InsertBlock`` runs.

    ``InsertBlock`` with an unknown name raises a bare COM error after the call
    has already been dispatched; refusing here names the block, costs one
    ``Blocks.Item`` probe and matches the headless engine's message. A layout
    block (``IsLayout``) cannot be inserted into itself — same refusal by name.
    Shared by ``block_insert`` and ``entity_create_block_ref``.
    """
    if not isinstance(name, str) or not name.strip():
        raise TypeError("block name must be a non-empty string")
    try:
        block = doc.Blocks.Item(name)
    except Exception as exc:
        raise ValueError(f"block {name!r} is not defined") from exc
    try:
        is_layout = bool(block.IsLayout)
    except Exception:
        is_layout = False
    if is_layout:
        raise ValueError(f"block {name!r} is a layout block and cannot be inserted")
    return block
```

In **both** `entity_create_block_ref` and `block_insert`, make the gate the first statement of `_sync` (before `mspace = _msp()`):

```python
        def _sync():
            _require_block_defined(_acad_doc(), name)
            mspace = _msp()
            ref = mspace.InsertBlock(
```

### Task 2: `block_explode` keeps ATTRIB text on both engines (the BURST rule)

Measured: ezdxf `Insert.virtual_entities()` yields the definition's geometry only (`['LINE']` for a block with a LINE and an ATTDEF), so the exploded symbol lost its tag value. ezdxf's own `explode_block_reference` documents the intended rule — "attached ATTRIB entities are converted to TEXT entities, this is the behavior of the BURST command of the AutoCAD Express Tools" — and reuses the ATTRIB's handle for the TEXT; the implementation below builds the TEXT explicitly (`msp.add_text` with the ATTRIB's WCS `insert`/`align_point`, `height`, `rotation`, `style`, `layer`, `halign`/`valign`) so no handle is recycled, and marks an invisible ATTRIB's TEXT invisible (`dxf.invisible = 1`) instead of making hidden data appear. Measured on the prototype: alignment `CENTER` survives, `doc.audit()` reports 0 errors, the TEXTs survive a write/read round trip.

On COM the brief's premise ("Explode already returns ATTDEF-derived TEXT") does not match AutoCAD's documented behaviour: EXPLODE keeps the attribute *definitions* (ATTDEF placeholders showing the tag name) and discards the values, and ActiveX `Explode()` additionally leaves the original reference in place. So the COM path applies the same BURST rule explicitly: read `GetAttributes()` before exploding, delete every `AcDbAttributeDefinition` the explode returns, `AddText` one TEXT per captured ATTRIB, delete the reference. The fake asserts exactly that call shape; the final group's live smoke (`scripts/smoke_settings_com.py`, Task 24) runs it once against AutoCAD.

**Files:**
- Modify: `backends/ezdxf_backend.py` (`block_explode` ≈ line 4507)
- Modify: `backends/com_backend.py` (`block_explode` ≈ line 3275)
- Modify: `server.py` (`block_explode` docstring ≈ line 3018)
- Modify: `discovery/aliases.py` (`block_explode` record ≈ line 222)
- Test: `tests/test_track_a_hardening.py` (append)

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: `block_explode(handle) -> {"ok": True, "exploded_handle": handle, "inserted_handles": [geometry handles], "attribute_texts": [one TEXT handle per ATTRIB], "backend": "ezdxf"|"com"}` on both engines. Refusal (unchanged type): `RuntimeError("Entity <h> is not a block reference (INSERT)")` before any write — on COM now checked on `ObjectName` instead of letting `Explode()` fail on a non-INSERT.

```python
    async def block_explode(self, handle) -> dict:
        def _sync():
            ent = self._get_entity(handle)
            if ent.dxftype() != "INSERT":
                raise RuntimeError(f"Entity {handle} is not a block reference (INSERT)")
            msp = self._msp()
            # Decompose: add individual entities to modelspace
            inserted = []
            for sub in ent.virtual_entities():
                sub_copy = sub.copy()
                msp.add_entity(sub_copy)
                inserted.append(sub_copy.dxf.handle)

```

with

```python
    #: ATTRIB DXF attributes carried onto the TEXT that replaces it on explode.
    #: ``insert``/``align_point`` are already WCS on an attached ATTRIB.
    _ATTRIB_TO_TEXT = (
        "layer",
        "color",
        "style",
        "height",
        "rotation",
        "width",
        "oblique",
        "halign",
        "valign",
        "text_generation_flag",
        "insert",
        "align_point",
    )

    async def block_explode(self, handle) -> dict:
        """Explode an INSERT into its members; ATTRIB values survive as TEXT.

        ``virtual_entities()`` yields the definition's geometry only — no
        ATTDEF, no ATTRIB — so an exploded tagged symbol used to lose its tag
        text silently. This is the BURST rule (Express Tools): every attached
        ATTRIB becomes a TEXT at the same WCS placement, height, rotation,
        style and layer, carrying the *value*; an invisible ATTRIB becomes an
        invisible TEXT rather than appearing. The new handles are reported as
        ``attribute_texts``, separate from the geometry's ``inserted_handles``.
        """

        def _sync():
            ent = self._get_entity(handle)
            if ent.dxftype() != "INSERT":
                raise RuntimeError(f"Entity {handle} is not a block reference (INSERT)")
            msp = self._msp()
            inserted = []
            for sub in ent.virtual_entities():
                sub_copy = sub.copy()
                msp.add_entity(sub_copy)
                inserted.append(sub_copy.dxf.handle)
            attribute_texts = []
            for attrib in ent.attribs:
                dxfattribs = {
                    key: attrib.dxf.get(key)
                    for key in self._ATTRIB_TO_TEXT
                    if attrib.dxf.hasattr(key)
                }
                text = msp.add_text(attrib.dxf.text, dxfattribs=dxfattribs)
                if attrib.is_invisible:
                    text.dxf.invisible = 1
                attribute_texts.append(text.dxf.handle)
            msp.delete_entity(ent)
            self._mark_dirty()
            return {
                "ok": True,
                "exploded_handle": handle,
                "inserted_handles": inserted,
                "attribute_texts": attribute_texts,
                "backend": "ezdxf",
            }

        return await self._async(_sync)
```

In `backends/com_backend.py`, replace the whole `block_explode` method

```python

        def _sync():

        return await self._run(_sync)
```

with

```python
    async def block_explode(self, handle) -> dict:
        """Explode an INSERT through ActiveX; ATTRIB values survive as TEXT.

        AutoCAD's EXPLODE keeps the attribute *definitions* (the tag names as
        ATTDEF entities) and discards the values; ActiveX ``Explode()`` does
        the same and, unlike the command, leaves the original reference in
        place. This is the BURST rule instead: each ATTRIB's value, placement,
        height, rotation and layer are read before the explode, the ATTDEFs
        the explode returns are deleted, one ``AddText`` per ATTRIB carries the
        value (an invisible ATTRIB becomes an invisible TEXT), and the
        reference itself is deleted. Unit-tested against a fake ActiveX
        surface; ``Explode()``'s return shape is the ActiveX documented one
        (an array of the new objects) and is exercised live by the settings
        smoke.
        """

        def _sync():
            doc = _acad_doc()
            ent = doc.HandleToObject(handle)
            if ent.ObjectName != "AcDbBlockReference":
                raise RuntimeError(f"Entity {handle} is not a block reference (INSERT)")
            captured = []
            try:
                attrs = ent.GetAttributes()
            except Exception as exc:
                log.debug("GetAttributes failed before explode: %s", exc)
                attrs = ()
            for attr in attrs:
                try:
                    invisible = bool(attr.Invisible)
                except Exception:
                    invisible = False
                captured.append(
                    {
                        "text": str(attr.TextString),
                        "insertion": tuple(attr.InsertionPoint),
                        "height": float(attr.Height),
                        "rotation": float(attr.Rotation),
                        "layer": str(attr.Layer),
                        "invisible": invisible,
                    }
                )
            exploded = ent.Explode()
            inserted = []
            for obj in exploded or ():
                if obj.ObjectName == "AcDbAttributeDefinition":
                    obj.Delete()  # the tag placeholder EXPLODE leaves; the value is below
                    continue
                inserted.append(str(obj.Handle))
            mspace = _msp()
            attribute_texts = []
            for item in captured:
                ins = item["insertion"]
                text = mspace.AddText(
                    item["text"],
                    _apoint(ins[0], ins[1], ins[2] if len(ins) > 2 else 0.0),
                    item["height"],
                )
                text.Rotation = item["rotation"]
                text.Layer = item["layer"]
                if item["invisible"]:
                    text.Visible = False
                attribute_texts.append(str(text.Handle))
            ent.Delete()
            _regen()
            return {
                "ok": True,
                "exploded_handle": handle,
                "inserted_handles": inserted,
                "attribute_texts": attribute_texts,
                "backend": "com",
            }

        return await self._run(_sync)
```

### Task 3: attribute values are validated before any write — `null` / bool / objects refused by tag, numbers written as plain text

Measured: `block_insert("TB", 0, 0, attributes={"TAG": None})` writes the text `None` (`True` → `True`, a dict → its repr) on both engines, and a non-mapping (`[("TAG", "x")]`) fails with `AttributeError` *after* `add_blockref` has written the INSERT (the modelspace grows, `_mark_dirty` is skipped). One shared validator in `backends/block_specs.py` runs before the write on both engines and in both tools.

**Files:**
- Modify: `backends/block_specs.py` (new `validate_attribute_values`, before `solid_vertices`)
- Modify: `backends/ezdxf_backend.py` (`block_insert`, `block_set_attributes`)
- Modify: `backends/com_backend.py` (`block_insert`, `block_set_attributes`)
- Modify: `server.py` (`block_insert`, `block_set_attributes` docstrings)
- Test: `tests/test_track_a_hardening.py` (append)

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: `validate_attribute_values(attributes, where="attributes") -> dict[str, str]` — `None` → `{}`; a non-dict → `TypeError("attributes must be an object of {TAG: value} pairs")`; a non-string/empty tag → `TypeError("attributes: every tag must be a non-empty string")`; a string value → itself after the module's `_one_line` rule (`TypeError("attributes['TAG'] must be a single line …")`); an int or finite float → `str(value)` (`101` → `"101"`, `2.5` → `"2.5"`); `bool`, `None`, list, dict → `TypeError("attributes['TAG'] must be a string or a number, got <type>")`; NaN/inf → `TypeError("attributes['TAG'] must be finite")`.
- Consumes: `_one_line` (same module, Track A Task 2).

- [ ] **Step 1: Append the failing tests**

```python
# tests/test_track_a_hardening.py (append)


# ── Task 3: attribute values are validated before any write ──────────────────


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "value, fragment",
    [
        (None, "NoneType"),
        (True, "bool"),
        ({"a": 1}, "dict"),
        ([1, 2], "list"),
        (float("nan"), "finite"),
        ("two\nlines", "single line"),
    ],
)
async def test_insert_refuses_a_non_text_attribute_value_by_tag(backend, value, fragment):
    _define_tagged_block(backend)
    before = len(list(backend._msp()))
    with pytest.raises(TypeError, match=r"attributes\['TAG'\]") as exc:
        await backend.block_insert("TB", 0, 0, attributes={"TAG": value})
    assert fragment in str(exc.value)
    assert len(list(backend._msp())) == before, "a refused insert must not write"


async def test_insert_refuses_a_non_mapping_before_writing(backend):
    _define_tagged_block(backend)
    before = len(list(backend._msp()))
    with pytest.raises(TypeError, match="attributes must be an object"):
        await backend.block_insert("TB", 0, 0, attributes=[("TAG", "x")])
    assert len(list(backend._msp())) == before


async def test_insert_writes_numbers_as_plain_text(backend):
    _define_tagged_block(backend)
    ref = await backend.block_insert("TB", 0, 0, attributes={"TAG": 101})
    assert await backend.block_get_attributes(ref.handle) == {"TAG": "101"}
    ref = await backend.block_insert("TB", 0, 0, attributes={"TAG": 2.5})
    assert await backend.block_get_attributes(ref.handle) == {"TAG": "2.5"}


async def test_set_attributes_refuses_by_tag_and_leaves_the_value_alone(backend):
    _define_tagged_block(backend)
    ref = await backend.block_insert("TB", 0, 0, attributes={"TAG": "P-101"})
    with pytest.raises(TypeError, match=r"attributes\['TAG'\] must be a string or a number"):
        await backend.block_set_attributes(ref.handle, {"TAG": None})
    assert await backend.block_get_attributes(ref.handle) == {"TAG": "P-101"}
    result = await backend.block_set_attributes(ref.handle, {"TAG": 7})
    assert result == {"ok": True, "updated_tags": ["TAG"]}
    assert await backend.block_get_attributes(ref.handle) == {"TAG": "7"}


async def test_com_insert_refuses_before_insert_block_and_writes_numbers_as_text(com):
    backend, document, space = com
    space.attributes = {"TAG": ""}
    with pytest.raises(TypeError, match=r"attributes\['TAG'\]"):
        await backend.block_insert("TB", 0, 0, attributes={"TAG": None})
    assert space.calls == [], "the refusal fires before InsertBlock"

    await backend.block_insert("TB", 0, 0, attributes={"TAG": 101})
    assert [name for name, _ in space.calls] == ["InsertBlock"]
    attr = space.inserted[0].GetAttributes()[0]
    assert attr.TextString == "101" and ("TextString", "101") in attr.writes


async def test_com_set_attributes_refuses_before_touching_the_reference(com):
    backend, document, space = com
    attr = _FakeObject("AcDbAttribute", "A1", TagString="TAG", TextString="P-101")
    calls = []

    def get_attributes():
        calls.append("GetAttributes")
        return (attr,)

    document.objects["2F"] = _FakeObject(
        "AcDbBlockReference", "2F", Name="TB", GetAttributes=get_attributes
    )
    with pytest.raises(TypeError, match=r"attributes\['TAG'\]"):
        await backend.block_set_attributes("2F", {"TAG": True})
    assert calls == [] and attr.TextString == "P-101"
    assert await backend.block_set_attributes("2F", {"TAG": 2.5}) == {
        "ok": True,
        "updated_tags": ["TAG"],
    }
    assert attr.TextString == "2.5"
```

Expected: 10 FAIL, 1 PASS. The six parametrized cases, both `set_attributes` tests and the COM insert test fail with `Failed: DID NOT RAISE TypeError` (`None` is written as `"None"`, `True` as `"True"`); `test_insert_refuses_a_non_mapping_before_writing` fails with `AttributeError: 'list' object has no attribute 'items'` — raised after the write, which is the defect. `test_insert_writes_numbers_as_plain_text` already passes (`str(101)`), and stays as the regression pin.

- [ ] **Step 3: The shared validator**

In `backends/block_specs.py`, insert before `def solid_vertices(...)`:

```python
def validate_attribute_values(attributes, where: str = "attributes") -> dict[str, str]:
    """``{TAG: value}`` for ``block_insert`` / ``block_set_attributes`` as the
    strings that will be written, refused before any write.

    ``str(value)`` used to be applied to whatever arrived, so a JSON ``null``
    became the text ``None``, ``true`` became ``True`` and an object became its
    Python repr — silently, on both engines. A string is taken as is (one line,
    the DXF rule ``_one_line`` enforces); an int or a finite float is written
    with ``str`` (``101`` → ``"101"``, ``2.5`` → ``"2.5"``); ``bool``, ``None``,
    lists and objects are refused by tag. ``None`` for the whole mapping means
    "no attributes".
    """
    if attributes is None:
        return {}
    if not isinstance(attributes, dict):
        raise TypeError(f"{where} must be an object of {{TAG: value}} pairs")
    out: dict[str, str] = {}
    for tag, value in attributes.items():
        if not isinstance(tag, str) or not tag.strip():
            raise TypeError(f"{where}: every tag must be a non-empty string")
        label = f"{where}[{tag!r}]"
        if isinstance(value, str):
            out[tag] = _one_line(value, label)
        elif isinstance(value, bool) or value is None or not isinstance(value, (int, float)):
            raise TypeError(f"{label} must be a string or a number, got {type(value).__name__}")
        else:
            if isinstance(value, float) and not math.isfinite(value):
                raise TypeError(f"{label} must be finite")
            out[tag] = str(value)
    return out
```

```python
    async def block_set_attributes(self, handle, attributes) -> dict:
        from backends.block_specs import validate_attribute_values

        values = validate_attribute_values(attributes)

        def _sync():
            ent = self._get_entity(handle)
            if ent.dxftype() != "INSERT":
                raise RuntimeError(f"Entity {handle} is not a block reference")
            updated = []
            for attrib in ent.attribs:
                tag = attrib.dxf.tag
                if tag in values:
                    attrib.dxf.text = values[tag]
                    updated.append(tag)
            self._mark_dirty()
            return {"ok": True, "updated_tags": updated}

        return await self._async(_sync)
```

    async def block_set_attributes(self, handle, attributes) -> dict:
        from backends.block_specs import validate_attribute_values

### Task 4: `block_define` refuses a non-finite base point and a missing primitive layer (unless `create_layers=True`)

Measured: `block_define("PID_NAN", CIRC, base_x=nan, base_y=inf)` returns `ok: True` and the BLOCK record carries `Vec3(nan, inf, 0)`; a primitive on `NOT_A_LAYER` is written with no layer-table entry and `doc.audit()` reports nothing. On COM `obj.Layer = name` for an unknown layer runs mid-loop after `Blocks.Add`, so a refusal there would leave the definition half written. Both engines now check the base point with the module's `_scalar` rule and probe the layer table before the first write; `create_layers=True` creates the missing layers (engine defaults) and reports them. The kwarg is optional, so every Track A caller (`engineering/pid/insert.py::ensure_block` passes `0.0, 0.0` on layer `0`) and the parent plan's Interfaces stay valid.

**Files:**
- Modify: `backends/block_specs.py` (new `validate_base_point`, before `validate_attribute_values`)
- Modify: `backends/contracts/blocks.py` (`block_define` signature + docstring)
- Modify: `backends/ezdxf_backend.py` (`block_define` ≈ line 4670)
- Modify: `backends/com_backend.py` (`block_define` ≈ line 3431)
- Modify: `server.py` (`block_define` tool: new parameter, docstring)
- Modify: `tests/test_block_define.py` (the pinned result dict gains `layers_created`)
- Test: `tests/test_track_a_hardening.py` (append)

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: `validate_base_point(base_x, base_y) -> tuple[float, float]` (`TypeError` naming `'base_x'` / `'base_y'`: non-number, bool, non-finite, overflowing int — `_scalar` gains the `OverflowError` guard, so every `block_define` scalar benefits). `block_define(name, entities, attdefs=None, base_x=0.0, base_y=0.0, overwrite=False, create_layers=False) -> {ok, name, entity_count, attdef_count, replaced, layers_created: [names], backend}`. Refusal: `ValueError("block_define: layer(s) 'A', 'B' do not exist in the drawing; create them with layer_create or pass create_layers=true")`, before `blocks.new` / `Blocks.Add`.
- Consumes: ezdxf `doc.layers` (`in` is case-insensitive) / `doc.layers.add(name)`; ActiveX `Layers.Item(name)` (raises for unknown) / `Layers.Add(name)`.

CIRC = [{"type": "circle", "cx": 0, "cy": 0, "r": 1}]
ON_LAYER = [{"type": "line", "x1": 0, "y1": 0, "x2": 1, "y2": 1, "layer": "NOT_A_LAYER"}]


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "kwargs, key",

        ({"base_x": float("nan")}, "base_x"),

async def test_define_refuses_a_bad_base_point_before_writing(backend, kwargs, key):
    with pytest.raises(TypeError, match=f"'{key}'"):

async def test_define_refuses_a_missing_layer_and_names_it(backend):
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="'NOT_A_LAYER' do not exist") as exc:
        await backend.block_define("PID_LYR", ON_LAYER)
    assert "create_layers=true" in str(exc.value)
    assert "PID_LYR" not in backend._doc.blocks
    assert "NOT_A_LAYER" not in backend._doc.layers


async def test_define_creates_the_layer_on_request_and_reports_it(backend):
    result = await backend.block_define("PID_LYR", ON_LAYER, create_layers=True)
    assert result["layers_created"] == ["NOT_A_LAYER"] and result["ok"] is True
    assert "NOT_A_LAYER" in backend._doc.layers
    member = next(iter(backend._doc.blocks.get("PID_LYR")))
    assert member.dxf.layer == "NOT_A_LAYER"


async def test_define_on_an_existing_layer_reports_nothing_created(backend):
    await backend.layer_create("PID-EQUIP")
    spec = [{**ON_LAYER[0], "layer": "PID-EQUIP"}]
    result = await backend.block_define("PID_OK", spec)
    assert result["layers_created"] == []
    result = await backend.block_define("PID_ZERO", CIRC)
    assert result["layers_created"] == [], "layer 0 always exists"


async def test_com_define_probes_layers_before_blocks_add(com):
    backend, document, space = com
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="'NOT_A_LAYER' do not exist"):
        await backend.block_define("PID_LYR", ON_LAYER)
    assert "PID_LYR" not in document.Blocks.blocks and document.Layers.added == []

    result = await backend.block_define("PID_LYR", ON_LAYER, create_layers=True)
    assert result["layers_created"] == ["NOT_A_LAYER"] and result["backend"] == "com"
    assert document.Layers.added == ["NOT_A_LAYER"]
    assert [name for name, _ in document.Blocks.blocks["PID_LYR"].calls] == ["AddLine"]


async def test_com_define_refuses_a_bad_base_point_before_blocks_add(com):
    backend, document, space = com
    with pytest.raises(TypeError, match="'base_y'"):
        await backend.block_define("PID_BASE", CIRC, base_y=float("nan"))
    assert "PID_BASE" not in document.Blocks.blocks
    result = await backend.block_define("PID_BASE", CIRC, base_x=1.5, base_y=2.5)
    assert document.Blocks.blocks["PID_BASE"].base_point == (1.5, 2.5, 0.0)
    assert result["layers_created"] == []

- [ ] **Step 3: The shared base-point validator (and `_scalar` learns about overflow)**

In `backends/block_specs.py`, `_scalar` let a 400-digit int escape as `OverflowError` (wave-2 finding) — replace

```python
    if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, (int, float)):
        raise TypeError(f"{where} must be a number, got {value!r}")
    number = float(value)
    if not math.isfinite(number):
        raise TypeError(f"{where} must be finite")
```

with

```python
    if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, (int, float)):
        raise TypeError(f"{where} must be a number, got {value!r}")
    try:
        number = float(value)
    except OverflowError as exc:  # a 400-digit JSON integer is an int, not a float
        raise TypeError(f"{where} must be finite") from exc
    if not math.isfinite(number):
        raise TypeError(f"{where} must be finite")
```

Then insert before `def validate_attribute_values(...)` (Task 3):

```python
def validate_base_point(base_x, base_y) -> tuple[float, float]:
    """``block_define``'s base point as finite floats; ``TypeError`` names the key.

    Only ``float()`` was applied before, so ``float("nan")`` was written into
    the BLOCK record headlessly and would reach AutoCAD as a NaN VARIANT.
    """
    return (
        _scalar(base_x, "block_define: 'base_x'"),
        _scalar(base_y, "block_define: 'base_y'"),
    )
```

        ``base_x``/``base_y`` (``TypeError`` naming it), a name clash without
        ``overwrite`` (``ValueError``), and a primitive ``layer`` that does not
        exist in the drawing (``ValueError`` listing the missing names) unless
        ``create_layers`` is true, in which case the missing layers are created
        with the engine's defaults and listed in ``layers_created``. Returns

- [ ] **Step 7: The tool grows the kwarg; the docstring names every refusal**

In `server.py`, `block_define` — replace

```python
    overwrite: Annotated[
        bool, "Replace the contents of an existing definition of this name"
    ] = False,
    ctx: Context = None,
) -> dict:
    """Create a block definition with attribute definitions from typed specs.

    The whole request is validated before anything is written: one malformed
    entry refuses the call and leaves no definition behind. `overwrite=true`
    replaces the *contents* of an existing block so its INSERTs keep pointing
    at the name and show the new geometry; `replaced` reports it. Geometry
    inside the block is ByBlock so the INSERT's layer supplies colour and
    lineweight. Insert with `block_insert(attributes={TAG: value})`.
    """
    await ctx.info(f"Defining block '{name}' from {len(entities)} primitives")
    return await _backend(ctx).block_define(name, entities, attdefs, base_x, base_y, overwrite)
```

with

```python
    overwrite: Annotated[
        bool, "Replace the contents of an existing definition of this name"
    ] = False,
    create_layers: Annotated[
        bool, "Create primitive layers that do not exist yet (default: refuse and name them)"
    ] = False,
    ctx: Context = None,
) -> dict:
    """Create a block definition with attribute definitions from typed specs.

    The whole request is validated before anything is written: one malformed
    entry refuses the call and leaves no definition behind. Refusals: a
    malformed primitive or ATTDEF (names `entities[i]`/`attdefs[i]` and the
    key), a non-finite base point (names `base_x`/`base_y`), a name clash
    without `overwrite`, an anonymous `*` name, and a primitive `layer` that
    is not in the drawing's layer table (names the missing layers) unless
    `create_layers=true`, which creates them and lists them in
    `layers_created`. `overwrite=true` replaces the *contents* of an existing
    block so its INSERTs keep pointing at the name and show the new geometry;
    `replaced` reports it. Geometry inside the block is ByBlock so the
    INSERT's layer supplies colour and lineweight. Insert with
    `block_insert(attributes={TAG: value})`.
    """
    await ctx.info(f"Defining block '{name}' from {len(entities)} primitives")
    return await _backend(ctx).block_define(
        name, entities, attdefs, base_x, base_y, overwrite, create_layers
    )
```

### Task 5: `pid_from_spec` — `_num` refuses non-finite numbers by path; `run_spec` rolls back on `BaseException`

Measured on main: `validate_spec` accepts `equipment[0].x = nan` and the INSERT is placed at `[nan, 0]` (the later `draw_line` refuses with `lines[0]: start.x is nan`, so the path named is the wrong one); `10**400` escapes as `OverflowError`; with `draw_line` monkeypatched to raise `asyncio.CancelledError`, `run_spec` leaves 5 entities in the drawing and `transaction_depth 1` — `except Exception` does not catch a `BaseException`, and on COM that is an open undo mark that refuses every later `pid_from_spec`.

**Files:**
- Modify: `engineering/pid/spec.py` (`_num` ≈ line 137; `run_spec` ≈ line 422)
- Modify: `server.py` (`pid_from_spec` docstring ≈ line 6780)
- Test: `tests/test_pid_from_spec.py` (append)

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: `_num(value, where) -> float` raising `ValueError(f"{where} must be a finite number")` for NaN/±inf/overflowing ints (the existing `must be a number` message for non-numbers is kept); `run_spec` rolls back on any `BaseException` and re-raises it.

- [ ] **Step 1: Append the failing tests**

```python
# tests/test_pid_from_spec.py (append)


# ── Track A hardening (track E wave 0, Task 5) ───────────────────────────────


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "mutate, fragment",
    [
        (lambda s: s["equipment"][0].update({"x": float("nan")}), "equipment[0].x"),
        (lambda s: s["valves"][0].update({"y": float("inf")}), "valves[0].y"),
        (lambda s: s["instruments"][0].update({"rotation": 10**400}), "instruments[0].rotation"),
        (lambda s: s["lines"][0].update({"stub": float("-inf")}), "lines[0].stub"),
    ],
)
async def test_non_finite_numbers_are_refused_by_path_before_any_write(backend, mutate, fragment):
    before = await backend.entity_count()
    spec = copy.deepcopy(EXAMPLE_SPEC)
    mutate(spec)
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="finite") as exc:
        await run_spec(backend, spec)
    assert fragment in str(exc.value)
    assert await backend.entity_count() == before
    assert (await backend.system_status())["transaction_depth"] == 0


async def test_a_cancellation_mid_run_rolls_the_sheet_back(backend, monkeypatch):
    """``CancelledError`` is a ``BaseException``; ``except Exception`` let the
    placed symbols stay inside an open transaction."""
    import asyncio

    from engineering.pid import spec as spec_module

    before = await backend.entity_count()

    async def cancelled(*args, **kwargs):
        raise asyncio.CancelledError()

    monkeypatch.setattr(spec_module, "draw_line", cancelled)
    with pytest.raises(asyncio.CancelledError):
        await run_spec(backend, EXAMPLE_SPEC)
    assert await backend.entity_count() == before, "the placed symbols were rolled back"
    assert (await backend.system_status())["transaction_depth"] == 0, "no open transaction"
```

Expected: 5 FAIL, 9 PASS — 14 collected items, counted by pytest item not by function: the file's existing nine (four plain tests plus `test_validation_names_the_path` parametrized five ways) keep passing; the five appended items (the four parametrized non-finite cases plus the cancellation test) fail. The `nan`/`inf` cases fail because the refusal names `lines[0]: start.x` / `lines[0]: end.y` (raised by `route()` after the symbol was placed and rolled back) instead of `equipment[0].x` / `valves[0].y`; `10**400` fails with `OverflowError: int too large to convert to float`; the stub case names `lines[0]: stub must be a finite length` (from `route`), not the spec path; the cancellation test fails `assert 5 == 0` — five symbols left in the drawing.

- [ ] **Step 3: Finite numbers, named by path**

In `engineering/pid/spec.py`, add `import math` after `import copy`:

```python
import copy
import math
from typing import TYPE_CHECKING
```

and replace

```python
def _num(value, where: str) -> float:
    if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, (int, float)):
        raise ValueError(f"{where} must be a number")
    return float(value)
```

with

```python
def _num(value, where: str) -> float:
    """A finite number, or a ``ValueError`` naming the path.

    ``float("nan")`` used to pass and be placed as an INSERT at ``[nan, 0]``;
    the transaction committed and ``build_graph`` then crashed on the drawing
    with no path in its message. Only in-process callers can deliver a
    non-finite (the MCP pipeline coerces them first), but ``run_spec`` is a
    public interface and its docstring promises nothing malformed reaches
    the drawing.
    """
    if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, (int, float)):
        raise ValueError(f"{where} must be a number")
    try:
        number = float(value)
    except OverflowError as exc:  # a 400-digit int is a number but not a float
        raise ValueError(f"{where} must be a finite number") from exc
    if not math.isfinite(number):
        raise ValueError(f"{where} must be a finite number")
    return number
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Roll back on anything**

In `run_spec`, replace

```python
            crossings_total += drawn["crossings"]
    except Exception:
        await backend.transaction_rollback()
        raise
    await backend.transaction_commit()
```

with

```python
            crossings_total += drawn["crossings"]
    except BaseException:
        # ``Exception`` alone let a client cancellation (``CancelledError`` is
        # a ``BaseException``) leave the placed symbols in the drawing inside
        # an open transaction — on COM with the undo mark still open, so every
        # later ``pid_from_spec`` was refused until a manual rollback.
        await backend.transaction_rollback()
        raise
    await backend.transaction_commit()
```

(`await` inside the handler is safe: once `CancelledError` has been thrown into the coroutine the task's cancel flag is cleared, so the rollback runs to completion before the re-raise; the test proves it.)

with

### Task 6: `pid_line_draw` — signal lines carry no fabricated number; a bubble exits towards the first waypoint

Measured on main: a pneumatic line from a bubble gets `line_number "1"`, a `"1"` TEXT on `PROCESS-LINE-TEXT`, and the next process line is seq 2 — `format_line_number` drops the empty fields and leaves the bare sequence, but an ISA-5.1 signal line has no pipe line number. And `_anchor` snaps a radial port's exit towards the *far end* even when `route` is a waypoint list: bubble (100,160) r 5, `route=[[130,160],[130,100]]` → first vertex `[100,155]`, first segment 4.93 mm from the centre (through the bubble). The dry run in `spec.py::_plan_lines` has the same anchor rule, so the pure helper `aim_points` serves both.

**Files:**
- Modify: `engineering/pid/lines.py` (new `aim_points`, before `label_placement`)
- Modify: `engineering/pid/drawlines.py` (`_anchor`, `draw_line`)
- Modify: `engineering/pid/spec.py` (`_plan_lines` anchor)
- Modify: `server.py` (`pid_line_draw` docstring), `discovery/aliases.py` (`pid_line_draw` synonyms)
- Modify: `tests/test_pid_deliverables.py::test_line_list_rows` (its pinned numbering was the defect)
- Modify: `benchmarks/adapters/autocad_mcp_pro.py::_task_pid_roundtrip` (two assertion lines — **final group's file**, see the group constraints; the change is additive to the region the final group edits — F Task 25 appends `_task_page_setup_truth` directly *after* this method — and is named in the commit message for the merge agent; the skeleton's H ownership row lists this edit)
- Test: `tests/test_pid_line_draw.py` (append)

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: `LineClass.kind` (`"process"` | `"signal"`), `_finite_point`, `snap_axis`.

# tests/test_pid_line_draw.py (append)


# ── Track A hardening (track E wave 0, Task 6) ───────────────────────────────


async def _bubble_and_valve(backend):
    cv = await place_symbol(backend, "globe", 100, 100, tag="FCV-1", actuator="diaphragm")
    fic = await place_symbol(
        backend, "instrument", 100, 160, tag="FIC-1", type="dcs", location="primary"
    )
    return cv, fic


async def test_signal_lines_carry_no_fabricated_number_and_do_not_consume_the_sequence(backend):
    """A signal line has no pipe line number: no auto number, no label, and the
    process sequence is left alone."""
    cv, fic = await _bubble_and_valve(backend)
    pump, vessel = await _pump_and_vessel(backend)
    signal = await draw_line(
        backend, {"handle": fic["handle"]}, {"handle": cv["handle"], "port": "signal"}, "pneumatic"
    )
    assert signal["line_number"] is None and signal["label_handle"] is None
    payload = await read_payload(backend, signal["handle"])
    assert payload["number"] is None and payload["seq"] is None
    labels = await backend.entity_count(type_filter="TEXT", layer_filter="PROCESS-LINE-TEXT")
    assert labels == 0, "no label text was written for the signal line"

    process = await draw_line(
        backend,
        {"handle": pump["handle"], "port": "discharge"},
        {"handle": vessel["handle"], "port": "N3"},
        size="100",
        service="P",
    )
    assert process["line_number"] == "100-P-1", "the signal line did not consume seq 1"
    assert (await read_payload(backend, process["handle"]))["seq"] == 1

    # A signal line given size/service still gets no auto number (kind rules).
    electric = await draw_line(
        backend, {"handle": fic["handle"]}, {"x": 160, "y": 160}, "electric", size="IA", service="S"
    )
    assert electric["line_number"] is None and electric["label_handle"] is None

    # A verbatim number is honoured and labelled, seq stays None.
    named = await draw_line(
        backend, {"handle": fic["handle"]}, {"x": 40, "y": 160}, "electric", line_number="IA-7"
    )
    assert named["line_number"] == "IA-7" and named["label_handle"]
    assert (await backend.entity_get(named["label_handle"])).properties["text"] == "IA-7"
    assert (await read_payload(backend, named["handle"]))["seq"] is None

    # The next process line is 2, not 4.
    second = await draw_line(
        backend,
        {"handle": vessel["handle"], "port": "N4"},
        {"x": 300, "y": 128},
        size="80",
        service="P",
    )

async def test_a_bubble_exit_aims_at_the_first_waypoint(backend):

- [ ] **Step 3: The pure aim rule**

In `engineering/pid/lines.py`, insert before `def label_placement(...)`:

```python
def aim_points(start, end, mode) -> tuple[Point, Point]:
    """What each end of a line aims at: the other end, or — with waypoints —
    the first waypoint for the start and the last one for the end.

    A radial (bubble) port leaves its circle along the axis towards its aim
    point. Aiming at the far end while the route is a waypoint list put the
    exit on the wrong side of the bubble, so the first segment cut through it.
    """
    if isinstance(mode, (list, tuple)) and len(mode) > 0:
        return _finite_point("waypoints[0]", mode[0]), _finite_point(
            f"waypoints[{len(mode) - 1}]", mode[-1]
        )
    return _finite_point("end", end), _finite_point("start", start)
```
